<div align="left"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="100.38px"/><col width="438.20px"/><tr height="84.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 31</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">LOCK</span><span class="Bold" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:Bold;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">:</span> FLASH_CR lock</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set only. When set, the FLASH_CR register is locked. It is cleared by hardware after detecting the unlock sequence.<br/>In case of an unsuccessful unlock operation, this bit remains set until the next system reset.</p></td></tr><tr height="114.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 30</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">OPTLOCK</span><span class="Bold" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:Bold;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">:</span> Options lock</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set only. When set, all bits concerning user option in FLASH_CR register and so option page are locked. This bit is cleared by hardware after detecting the unlock sequence. The LOCK bit must be cleared before doing the unlock sequence for OPTLOCK bit.</p><p class="TableIndent">In case of an unsuccessful unlock operation, this bit remains set until the next reset.</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 29</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="52.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 28</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">SEC_PROT1</span>: Securable memory area protection bit.<br/>This bit is set to lock the access to the securable memory area. It is set by software when exiting the securable memory area, and can only be written once. </p></td></tr><tr height="86.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 27</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">OBL_LAUNCH</span>: Force the option byte loading</p><p class="TableIndent">When set to 1, this bit forces the option byte reloading. This bit is cleared only when the option byte loading is complete. It cannot be written if OPTLOCK is set.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Option byte loading complete</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Option byte loading requested</p></td></tr><tr height="85.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 26</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">RDERRIE</span>: PCROP read error interrupt enable</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit enables the interrupt generation when the RDERR bit in the FLASH_SR is set to 1.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: PCROP read error interrupt disabled<br/>1: PCROP read error interrupt enabled</p></td></tr><tr height="85.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 25</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">ERRIE</span>: Error interrupt enable</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit enables the interrupt generation when the OPERR bit in the FLASH_SR is set to 1.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: OPERR error interrupt disabled<br/>1: OPERR error interrupt enabled</p></td></tr><tr height="86.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 24</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">EOPIE</span>: End of operation interrupt enable</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit enables the interrupt generation when the EOP bit in the FLASH_SR is set to 1.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: EOP Interrupt disabled</p><p class="TableIndent">1: EOP Interrupt enabled</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 23:19</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="56.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 18</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">FSTPG</span>: Fast programming</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Fast programming disabled</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Fast programming enabled</p></td></tr><tr height="70.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 17</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">OPTSTRT</span>: Options modification start</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit triggers an options operation when set.</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set only by software, and is cleared when the BSY bit is cleared in FLASH_SR.</p></td></tr><tr height="100.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 16</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">START</span>: Start </p><p class="TableIndent">This bit triggers an erase operation when set. If MER1, MER2 and PER bits are reset and the STRT bit is set, an unpredictable behavior may occur without generating any error flag. This condition should be forbidden.</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set only by software, and is cleared when the BSY bit is cleared in FLASH_SR.</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 15:11</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="105.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 10:3</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">PNB[7:0]</span>: Page number selection</p><p class="TableBodyL">These bits select the page to erase:</p><p class="TableIndent">00000000:   page 0</p><p class="TableIndent">00000001:   page 1</p><p class="TableIndent">...</p><p class="TableIndent">11111111:   page 255</p></td></tr><tr height="40.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 2</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">MER1</span>: Mass erase</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit triggers the mass erase (all user pages) when set.</p></td></tr><tr height="56.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 1</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">PER</span>: Page erase</p><p class="TableIndent">0: page erase disabled</p><p class="TableIndent">1: page erase enabled</p></td></tr><tr height="56.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">PG</span>: Programming</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Flash programming disabled</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Flash programming enabled</p></td></tr></table></div>


### 4.7.7 Flash ECC register (FLASH\_ECCR)

Address offset: 0x18

Reset value: 0x0000 0000

Access: no wait state when no flash memory operation is on going, word, half-word and byte access

<div align="left"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="101.11px"/><col width="437.47px"/><tr height="70.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 31</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">ECCD</span>: ECC detection</p><p class="TableIndent">Set by hardware when two ECC errors have been detected (only if ECCC/ECCD are previously cleared). When this bit is set, a NMI is generated.</p><p class="TableIndent">Cleared by writing 1.</p></td></tr><tr height="85.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 30</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">ECCC</span>: ECC correction</p><p class="TableIndent">Set by hardware when one ECC error has been detected and corrected (only if ECCC/ECCC2/ECCD/ECCD2 are previously cleared). An interrupt is generated if ECCCIE is set.</p><p class="TableIndent">Cleared by writing 1.</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 29:25</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="88.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 24</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">ECCCIE</span>: ECC correction interrupt enable</p><p class="TableIndent">0: ECCC interrupt disabled</p><p class="TableIndent">1: ECCC interrupt enabled.</p><p class="TableBodyL">This bit enables the interrupt generation when the ECCC bit in the FLASH_ECCR register is set.</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 23</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="54.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 22</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">SYSF_ECC</span>: System flash ECC fail</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit indicates that the ECC error correction or double ECC error detection is located in the System flash.</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 21:19</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="54.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 18:0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">ADDR_ECC[18:0]</span>: ECC fail address</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit indicates which address in the flash memory is concerned by the ECC error correction or by the double ECC error detection.</p></td></tr></table></div>


### 4.7.8 Flash option register (FLASH\_OPTR)

Address offset: 0x20

Reset value: 0xFXXX XXXX. Register bits are loaded with values from flash memory at OBL.

Access: no wait state when no flash memory operation is on going, word, half-word and byte access

<div align="left"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="101.11px"/><col width="437.47px"/><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 31</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="56.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 30</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">IRHEN</span>: Internal reset holder enable bit </p><p class="TableIndent">0: Internal resets are propagated as simple pulse on NRST pin </p><p class="TableIndent">1: Internal resets drives NRST pin low until it is seen as low level</p></td></tr><tr height="117.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 29:28</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">NRST_MODE[1:0]:</span></p><p class="TableIndent">00: Reserved</p><p class="TableIndent">01: Reset Input only: a low level on the NRST pin generates system reset, internal RESET not propagated to the NSRT pin</p><p class="TableIndent">10: GPIO: standard GPIO pad functionality, only internal RESET possible </p><p class="TableIndent">11: Bidirectional reset: NRST pin configured in reset input/output mode (legacy mode) </p></td></tr><tr height="56.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 27</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">nBOOT0:</span> nBOOT0 option bit</p><p class="TableIndent">0: nBOOT0 = 0</p><p class="TableIndent">1: nBOOT0 = 1</p></td></tr><tr height="56.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 26</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">nSWBOOT0:</span> Software BOOT0</p><p class="TableIndent">0: BOOT0 taken from the option bit nBOOT0</p><p class="TableIndent">1: BOOT0 taken from PB8/BOOT0 pin</p></td></tr><tr height="54.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 25</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">CCMSRAM_RST</span><span class="Bold" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:Bold;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">:</span> CCM SRAM Erase when system reset</p><p class="TableIndent">0: CCM SRAM erased when a system reset occurs<br/>1: CCM SRAM is not erased when a system reset occurs</p></td></tr><tr height="54.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 24</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">SRAM_PE</span><span class="Bold" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:Bold;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">:</span> SRAM1 and CCM SRAM parity check enable</p><p class="TableIndent">0: SRAM1 and CCM SRAM parity check enable<br/>1: SRAM1 and CCM SRAM parity check disable</p></td></tr><tr height="69.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 23</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">nBOOT1</span><span class="Bold" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:Bold;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">:</span><span class="Bold" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;"> Boot configuration</span></p><p class="TableIndent">Together with the BOOT0 pin, this bit selects boot mode from the flash main memory, SRAM1 or the System memory. Refer to <a href="RM0440_MAP_UserSpec.htm#XREF_50735__2_5_Boot_configuration3" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;font-kerning:normal;font-stretch:normal;color:#0000ff;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">Section 2.6: Boot configuration</a>.</p></td></tr><tr height="73.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 22</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL" style="font-weight:Bold;">PB4_PUPEN: <span style="font-weight:normal;">PB4 pull-up enable</span> </p><p class="TableIndent">0: USB power delivery dead-battery enabled/ NJTRST pull-up deactivated</p><p class="TableIndent">1: USB power delivery dead-battery disabled/ NJTRST pull-up activated </p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>Only for Category 4 devices (otherwise Reserved)</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits: 21:20</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="54.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 19</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL" style="font-weight:Bold;"><span class="Bold" style="font-family : Arial ; font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">WWDG_SW:</span><span class="Bold" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;"> Window watchdog selection</span></p><p class="TableIndent">0: Hardware window watchdog<br/>1: Software window watchdog</p></td></tr><tr height="54.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 18</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL" style="font-weight:Bold;"><span class="Bold" style="font-family : Arial ; font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">IWDG_STDBY:</span><span class="Bold" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;"> Independent watchdog counter freeze in Standby mode</span></p><p class="TableIndent">0: <span class="Bold" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">Independent watchdog </span>counter is frozen in Standby mode<br/>1: <span class="Bold" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">Independent watchdog counter is running </span>in Standby mode</p></td></tr><tr height="54.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 17</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL" style="font-weight:Bold;"><span class="Bold" style="font-family : Arial ; font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">IWDG_STOP:</span><span class="Bold" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;"> Independent watchdog counter freeze in Stop mode</span></p><p class="TableIndent">0: <span class="Bold" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">Independent watchdog </span>counter is frozen in Stop mode<br/>1: <span class="Bold" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">Independent watchdog counter is running </span>in Stop mode</p></td></tr><tr height="54.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 16</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL" style="font-weight:Bold;"><span class="Bold" style="font-family : Arial ; font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">IWDG_SW:</span><span class="Bold" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;"> Independent watchdog selection</span></p><p class="TableIndent">0: Hardware independent watchdog<br/>1: Software independent watchdog</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 15</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="54.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 14</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL" style="font-weight:Bold;"><span class="Bold" style="font-family : Arial ; font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">nRST_SHDW</span></p><p class="TableIndent">0: Reset generated when entering the Shutdown mode<br/>1: No reset generated when entering the Shutdown mode</p></td></tr><tr height="54.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 13</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL" style="font-weight:Bold;"><span class="Bold" style="font-family : Arial ; font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">nRST_STDBY</span></p><p class="TableIndent">0: Reset generated when entering the Standby mode<br/>1: No reset generate when entering the Standby mode</p></td></tr><tr height="54.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 12</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL" style="font-weight:Bold;"><span class="Bold" style="font-family : Arial ; font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">nRST_STOP</span></p><p class="TableIndent">0: Reset generated when entering the Stop mode<br/>1: No reset generated when entering the Stop mode</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 11</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="129.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits10:8</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">BOR_LEV</span><span class="Bold" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:Bold;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">:</span> BOR reset level</p><p class="TableIndent">These bits contain the VDD supply level threshold that activates/releases the reset.</p><p class="TableIndent">000: BOR level 0. Reset level threshold is around 1.7 V<br/>001: BOR level 1. Reset level threshold is around 2.0 V<br/>010: BOR level 2. Reset level threshold is around 2.2 V<br/>011: BOR level 3. Reset level threshold is around 2.5 V<br/>100: BOR level 4. Reset level threshold is around 2.8 V</p></td></tr><tr height="101.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 7:0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">RDP</span><span class="Bold" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:Bold;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">:</span> Read protection level</p><p class="TableIndent">0xAA: Level 0, read protection not active<br/>0xCC: Level 2, chip read protection active<br/>Others: Level 1, memories read protection active</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>Take care about PCROP_RDP configuration in Level 1. Refer to <a href="Embedded_flash_memory_%28FLASH%29_for_category_2_devices.htm#XREF_94262__Read_protection13" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;font-kerning:normal;font-stretch:normal;color:#0000ff;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">Level 1: Read protection</a> for more details.</p></td></tr></table></div>


### 4.7.9 Flash PCROP1 Start address register (FLASH\_PCROP1SR)

Address offset: 0x24

Reset value: 0xFFFF XXXX

Register bits are loaded with values from flash memory at OBL.

Access: no wait state when no flash memory operation is on going, word access.

<div align="left"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="105.30px"/><col width="433.28px"/><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 31:16</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="40.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 15:0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span class="Helv7" style="font-weight:Bold;font-size : 9.00pt;background-color:transparent;">PCROP1_STRT[15:0]</span><span style="font-weight:Bold;">:</span> PCROP area start offset</p><p class="TableIndent">PCROP1_STRT contains the first double-word of the PCROP area.</p></td></tr></table></div>


### 4.7.10 Flash PCROP1 End address register (FLASH\_PCROP1ER)

Address offset: 0x28

Reset value: 0xX000 XXXX

Register bits are loaded with values from flash memory at OBL.

Access: no wait state when no flash memory operation is on going, word, half-word access. PCROP\_RDP bit can be accessed with byte access.

<div align="left"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="105.30px"/><col width="433.28px"/><tr height="116.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 31</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span class="Helv7" style="font-weight:Bold;font-size : 9.00pt;background-color:transparent;">PCROP_RDP</span><span style="font-weight:Bold;">:</span> PCROP area preserved when RDP level decreased</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set only. It is reset after a full mass erase due to a change of RDP from Level 1 to Level 0.</p><p class="TableIndent"><span class="Helv7" style="font-size : 9.00pt;background-color:transparent;">0: PCROP area is not erased when the RDP level is decreased from Level 1 to Level 0</span>. </p><p class="TableIndent">1: <span class="Helv7" style="font-size : 9.00pt;background-color:transparent;">PCROP area is erased when the RDP level is decreased from Level 1 to Level 0</span> (full mass erase).</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 30:16</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="40.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 15:0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span class="Helv7" style="font-weight:Bold;font-size : 9.00pt;background-color:transparent;">PCROP1_END[15:0]</span><span style="font-weight:Bold;">:</span> PCROP area end offset</p><p class="TableIndent">PCROP1_END contains the last double-word of the PCROP area.</p></td></tr></table></div>


### 4.7.11 Flash WRP area A address register (FLASH\_WRP1AR)

Address offset: 0x2C

Reset value: 0x00XX 00XX

Register bits are loaded with values from flash memory at OBL.

Access: no wait state when no flash memory operation is on going, word, half-word and byte access

### 4.7.12 Flash WRP area B address register (FLASH\_WRP1BR)

Address offset: 0x30

Reset value: 0x00XX 00XX

Register bits are loaded with values from flash memory at OBL.

Access: no wait state when no flash memory operation is on going, word, half-word and byte access

### 4.7.13 Flash securable area register (FLASH\_SEC1R)

Address offset: 0x70

Reset value: 0xFFFX FXXX

Access: no wait state when no flash memory operation is on going, word, half-word and byte access

<div align="left"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="105.30px"/><col width="433.28px"/><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 31:17</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="86.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 16</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span class="Helv7" style="font-weight:Bold;font-size : 9.00pt;background-color:transparent;">BOOT_LOCK</span><span style="font-weight:Bold;">:</span> used to force boot from user flash area</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Boot based on the pad/option bit configuration</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Boot forced from main flash memory</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit can only be changed in RDP level 0 or when doing regression from RDP level 1 to RDP level 0.</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 15:9</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="85.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 8:0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">SEC_SIZE1[8:0]</span>: sets the number of pages used in the securable area.</p><p class="TableIndent">Securable area starts at 0x0800 0000 and its size is SEC_SIZE1 * page size. This field can be changed in RDP level0 only. </p><p class="TableIndent">Any attempt to modify in RDP level1 silently fails, and does not change register value.</p></td></tr></table></div>
